# Consolidated reading decisions

The scan images supplied for cote 156-6 are the authority. C is the supplied Codex draft; H is the Commutator project draft, commit `aef7db2`. An entry is a localized decision, not a count of changed characters. Pure differences of markup are excluded. A retained or resolved doubt may have a decision row with neither textual-difference flag set. Pixel boxes refer to the original whole-page image, before enlargement or rotation.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 017, (i), initial quantified formula (017-01): \forall x\in\unsure{M|\mathcal{M}}. The first crossed letter is small relative to the quantifier and M. M has two upright peaks with a curved entry; the glyph does not establish a separate calligraphic alphabet. M is retained first, with alphabet uncertainty stated. Main review: Quantifier followed by small crossed x, membership and two-peaked M. x is visibly smaller than M; preserve lowercase, alphabet of M uncertain.
- Scan 017, (i), family of sets containing the point (017-03): {\unsure{\mathcal{F}|\mathfrak{F}|\mathscr{F}}}^{\unsure{x|X}}. The family letter has a swept top and curled foot, unlike the ordinary F later in the same crop. The superscript is a tall crossed X; its case is not certain enough to suppress x as an alternative. The printed alphabet is not uniquely settled by handwriting. Main review: Ornamental F with leftward curved cap, central crossbar and hooked foot, superscript crossed x. Same family in following F membership; cal/scr/frak ambiguity remains.
- Scan 017, (ii), application sign (017-11): X\mathrel{\text{---}}F_X. The connecting ink is a single horizontal stroke with a small down-turn at the right end. It has neither a right arrowhead nor a left mapsto bar. Main review: Between X and F_X a single unheaded horizontal stroke ends with downward pen exit, no arrowhead or mapsto initial bar. Use plain dash relation.
- Scan 017, insertion above struck words (017-16): \inserted{forment un ens}. The inserted first word begins with a tall looped f and has the separate descending/ascending group of forment; it is followed by un and the compressed ens ending. It sits above the canceled sont des. Main review: Upper forment un ens is live above bracket; lower sont des crossed by horizontal stroke. Underlying letters not all secure, retain doubt for deletion.
- Scan 017, Dès lors, live de parties fermées (017-17): de parties fermées. The main baseline continues with de, then a descender p and the compressed parties, followed by the long looped f and accented ending of fermées. These words are not crossed out; the long curved line belongs to the interlinear insertion. The first crop cut the initial de, so the linked full crop was widened. Main review: parties fermées lies live on original line, with tall p/t and long f plus accent; De plus, on suppose inserted below is separate. H interleaves these wrongly.
- Scan 017, interlinear containing clause (017-19): \inserted{contenant les $M_{\leq x}$, $x\in M$\unsure{!?|!|?}}. Con- and the crossed tall t of contenant are legible, followed by les and both mathematical terms. A long curved leader encloses/joins this inserted passage after de M. The first crop clipped the upper punctuation, hence the necessary widened crop. Main review: Interlinear containing phrase reads contenant les M_{≤x}, x∈M with small x’s. Thin enclosing guide not deletion; final punctuation has an upright/loop plus questioned hook; keep !?|!|? uncertainty.
- Scan 017, replacement insertion De plus, on suppose (017-28): \inserted{De plus on suppose}. Entre la terminaison de plus et le petit groupe on, le long retour inférieur appartient au f de fermées situé au-dessus. Aucun trait détaché de virgule ne se voit à la ligne de l'ajout ; retirer la virgule que la première lecture lui attribuait.
- Scan 017, lower paragraph, à des conditions (017-37): à des. There is a separate small à before a looped initial d in des. The crop does not support H’s single les. Main review: Visible sat/is/f loop then small ais and detached full stop; à and des follow clearly. Keep satisfais. à des, no expanded satisfaisant or les.
- Scan 017, first overwritten page reference (017-41): \deleted{\unsure{52|53}}53. The live right digit has the upper and lower rightward lobes of 3. A lower/underlying turn can belong to the old 2, but the old digit is too overwritten to settle; its uncertainty remains. Main review: Word before digits begins p and has looped g; ending reduced, pages|page retained. Written live digits 53, 54, 58: 3 double-curved, 4 upright/crossstroke, 8 two loops. Overwritten earlier 52|53 and 56|54 cannot be fixed confidently.
- Scan 017, second overwritten page reference (017-42): \deleted{\unsure{56|54}}54. The live last digit has a diagonal entry, crossing stroke and upright of 4. A lower loop survives under it, compatible with old 6, but that older numeral remains doubtful. Main review: Word before digits begins p and has looped g; ending reduced, pages|page retained. Written live digits 53, 54, 58: 3 double-curved, 4 upright/crossstroke, 8 two loops. Overwritten earlier 52|53 and 56|54 cannot be fixed confidently.
- Scan 017, last page reference (017-43): 58. A tall 5 with upper and lower turns precedes a closed double-loop 8. This is a two-digit number. Main review: Word before digits begins p and has looped g; ending reduced, pages|page retained. Written live digits 53, 54, 58: 3 double-curved, 4 upright/crossstroke, 8 two loops. Overwritten earlier 52|53 and 56|54 cannot be fixed confidently.
- Scan 017, interlinear et before avec (017-53): \inserted{et}. The small addition has a shallow e followed by a tall t; its detached crossing bar lies above. The descending placement stroke goes to the space after partie vide and before avec. It is not qui after the family letter. Main review: Family F with curled cap, central crossbar and foot; below it live inserted et, with looped e and tall t. Not qui.
- Scan 017, compatibility criterion, F,G in family-like glyph (017-59): \unsure{\mathcal{J}|\mathcal{F}}. The glyph has a swept top and large closed lower leftward loop, without the usual separate middle crossbar. This visually favours J, but the family F remains possible as a malformed stroke. Mathematical expectation is not used to erase the difference. Main review: Lower F,G membership glyph has long cap/stem and closed lower loop, unlike the usual open-foot family F. J is visually plausible, keep J|F uncertainty rather than repair by context.
- Scan 017, compatibility criterion, quantified point pair (017-60): \forall x\in F,\ \unsure{y|\forall y}\in G. The x after the first quantifier is small. The second variable has a loop and crossing strokes that may include an overlapping quantifier; there is no clear separated ∀ and y. C’s alternatives remain. Main review: First ∀ is separate from x. Second glyph combines long descending y with crossing curved stroke; ∀y possible but not separable. Keep y|∀y, avoid supplying quantifier by syntax.
- Scan 017, small dotted sign before first principal lower set (017-61): \unsure{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\cdots}{\scriptscriptstyle v}|\overset{\scriptscriptstyle\cdots}{\scriptscriptstyle\vee}}. A small two-limbed v-shaped mark stands immediately left of the M entry, with three small dark marks above it. It is not safely identifiable as ordinary punctuation or a correction sign, so it is retained with C’s uncertainty. Main review: Before first M a small v-shaped stroke with three detached upper marks; keep uncertain dotted v/vee rather than omit or invent an operator. M≤x ∪ M≤y are clear, y long descender.
- Scan 018, display (1.34), indexed stratum symbol (018-33): \unsure{x_i|X_i}. The relation has a separate lower bar, followed by two crossing x strokes and a distinctly lower dotted i. The tiny x is compatible with lower case; there is no independent comparison on this page that conclusively fixes its case. Candidate keeps C’s lower-case first reading and records the upper-case alternative. Main review: Long entry stroke, two high peaks and deep trough identify M; subscript has angular less-than and detached lower bar, then x with distinctly lowered dotted i: M_{≤x_i}.
- Scan 019, Second display, left number (019-05): (1.36). Le dernier chiffre part du haut à droite, décrit une longue courbe descendante à gauche puis se retourne en petite boucle basse vers le haut. Ce parcours est un 6 ouvert ; on ne voit ni le décrochement supérieur ni la panse droite d'un 5. Le point après 1 et le 3 sont distincts. Le doute 5 est levé sur le tracé, sans recours à la suite des numéros.
- Scan 019, Second display, final operand and mark (019-08): Y'. A separate short near-vertical stroke is above the final operand’s right shoulder. It is a prime, unlike the lower comma after the first Y in display_XY. The double-shaft implication arrow, X prime, relation, and final Y prime are all visible in the linked component. Main review: Two shafts and right arrowhead; X prime has a detached upper tick, final Y has a detached near-upright tick at mid-upper height. Y prime favored, not trailing comma. Same compatibility glyph.
- Scan 019, Correspondence paragraph, plus celle… (019-19): celle\deleted{s d'être}. The letters of celle stand wholly before the horizontal strike. A separate s begins the struck group; then d, apostrophe, the accented ê and tall t are visible under that same strike. The cancellation starts at s and crosses d’être, leaving celle live. Main review: celle is live, ending before separate s-like double curve. Strike starts at that s and crosses d’être; d upright/loop, apostrophe, circumflex and t are visible. Do not strike celle, and do not read disant.
- Scan 019, Order sign after la donnée de (019-27): \underset{M}{\leqslant}. The upper sign is a left-pointing inequality with a distinct lower stroke slanting parallel to its lower limb. The two-peak ambient label is centered immediately beneath the sign, not at its lower right. Both shape and placement are preserved. Main review: Single angle with parallel detached lower rule, M-like paired peaks centered directly beneath. It is an underset, not a right-hand subscript.
- Scan 019, Last paragraph, struck word and next live word (019-35): \deleted{\unsure{Comme}} Elle. The cancellation crosses the C, low o and flattened mme, then ends before the separate capital E. The following Elle has a clear capital E and two l loops and is live. There is no extra struck elle between these groups. Main review: Cancellation crosses Comme only, ending before the new tall E. C/o and remaining low m humps suggest Comme, but merged central letters justify retained doubt. Live Elle has tall retraced E followed by two high l loops and low e.

Plain M is favored and the alphabet is marked at this first occurrence. Main review: Omb(F) = {X ∈ M |: open O, low m and tall b; F straight cap, X crosses centrally. Ambient M has two narrow high peaks and deep valley, matching plain M.

- Scan 021, (*) domain family (021-01): \unsure{\mathcal{F}|\mathfrak{F}|\mathscr{F}}. A top returning loop, long leftward upper arm, separate lower arm, and descending stem distinguish this family sign from the ordinary operands. The exact calligraphic/Gothic/script alphabet is not determined by this one handwritten form. Calligraphic remains first, following the provisional notation while preserving both other candidates.
- Scan 021, (*) target ambient letter (021-02): \unsure{M|\mathcal{M}}. The letter has two upright peaks, a curved trough, a rising entry and a short returning foot. It is recognizably M but does not securely distinguish a plain handwritten capital from a calligraphic alphabet. The first-occurrence ambiguity is retained; plain M remains first.
- Scan 021, injectivity insertion, first shadow operator (021-04): \unsure{\mathrm{Om}|\mathrm{Omb}}(X). The first occurrence visibly has a round O, a flattened low m trace, then the opening parenthesis; I cannot distinguish a separate b ascender. The later Omb in the same insertion has a conspicuous detached upright b. Om is therefore the literal preferred reading here, with Omb retained because the writing is cramped. This is not normalized to the later spelling.
- Scan 021, injectivity insertion, operator before (F) (021-06): \mathrm{Multom}. M, the low u, looped l, tall t, rounded o and flattened m are visible. The m ends in a small downhook immediately before (F); there is no independent b upright or i before o. Multom is retained as the locally written short spelling rather than expanded to Multomb.
- Scan 021, (1.38), left operand of first inequality (021-09): \deleted{F}G. The thin original F has an upright and two bars, while the darker outer curve and lower right return form the final G over it. Both layers are recoverable and must be retained.

The extended first sa reading remains plausible, but the flattened joins do not securely distinguish those two initial letters from a broad v entry. Add the blind reader's shorter voir as an alternative, retaining savoir first; the word is not treated as an abbreviation. |
| Slanted margin, small mark below the strict comparison | < | \underset{\cdot}{<} | medium | The relation has one clean leftward angle and no lower equality bar. A separate short dark mark sits below and slightly left of its vertex, comparable in darkness with the lettering. Its offset position does not establish a deliberate dotted relation rather than incidental pen contact. Keep plain < as the primary reading and record the visible dotted alternative, without assigning it a new mathematical meaning. |
| Inserted qualification, article before relation | la | no separate word (the second reading omits la) | medium | The wider attachment shows et followed by a long loop and short upward return before relation. That loop is compatible with the inserted la, but its upper join coincides with the l course of relation on the main line above; a separate a is not plainly resolved at its lower exit. The second reader omits la. Keep the plausible word in place under unsure rather than assert either a certain extra word or a certain omission. |
